| 324 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1789 | The Guillotine Debate: Does Consciousness Survive | Joseph-Ignace Guillotin, Paris | Physicians hotly debate whether severed heads retain visual awareness and motor feeling for seconds post-drop. | `bci_ethics` |
| 325 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1791 | Galvani: De Viribus Electricitatis Birth of Bioelectricity | Luigi Galvani, Bologna | Publishes landmark treatise asserting living tissue generates intrinsic animal electricity via neural fluid. | `bci_tech` |
| 326 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1791 | Johann Christian Reil: Coining the Term Psychiatry | Johann Christian Reil, Univ. of Halle | Establishes physiological medicine of the mind and later describes the Insula (Island of Reil) in deep cortex. | `brain_history` |
| 327 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1792 | Alessandro Volta: Attacking Galvanis Animal Electricity | Alessandro Volta, Univ. of Pavia | Rejects Galvani, insisting electricity originates from chemical contact between dissimilar metals, not nerves. | `brain_mysteries` |
| 328 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1793 | Eusebio Valli: Nerve Poisoning Blocks Bioelectric Twitch | Eusebio Valli, Pisa & Pavia | Applies opium and curare-like toxins to frog nerves, studying progressive proximal-to-distal loss of excitability. | `brain_basics` |
| 329 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1793 | Matthew Baillie: Morbid Anatomy of the Human Brain | Matthew Baillie, London | First pathological anatomy textbook classifying brain tumors, hydatids, stroke cavities, and dural ossification. | `brain_health` |
| 330 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1793 | Philippe Pinel: Unchaining Psychiatric Patients at Bicetre | Philippe Pinel, Paris | Liberates insane from iron chains in asylum, treating mania and melancholia as organic cerebral illnesses. | `brain_health` |
| 331 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1794 | Galvani: Muscle-to-Nerve Contraction Without Any Metals | Luigi Galvani, Bologna | Triumphant rebuttal: touching cut nerve directly to bare injured muscle causes twitch, proving bioelectricity! | `brain_basics` |
| 332 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1794 | John Dalton: First Paper on Congenital Colorblindness | John Dalton, Manchester Literary Society | Analyzes his own deuteranopia red-green blindness; preserved eye DNA later confirms missing middle-wave cone. | `brain_basics` |
| 333 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1794 | Volta: The Bimetallic Contact Voltage Serier | Alessandro Volta, Pavia | Arranges metals in electromotive series, proving voltage increases with contact between zinc and silver discs. | `bci_tech` |
| 334 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1795 | Sommerring: Organ of the Soul in Ventricular Fluid Debate | Samuel Thomas von Sommerring, Gottingen | Locates sensorium commune in ventricular fluid; philosopher Immanuel Kant writes sharp refutation. | `brain_mysteries` |
| 335 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1796 | Franz Joseph Gall: Phrenology Lectures Begin in Vienna | Franz Joseph Gall, Vienna | Pioneers cranioscopy: skull bumps reflect underlying cerebral development of 27 distinct moral faculties. | `brain_history` |
| 336 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1796 | Gall: Proving White Matter is Composed of Axon Bundles | Franz Joseph Gall & Johann Spurzheim | Dissects brain from below, proving cortex gives rise to descending white matter fiber tracts to spinal cord. | `brain_basics` |
| 337 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1796 | William Tuke: Founding the York Retreat for Moral Treatment | William Tuke, Society of Friends York | Replaces bloodletting and shackles with quiet gardens, occupational therapy, and dignity for psychiatric patients. | `bci_ethics` |
| 338 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1797 | Alexander von Humboldt: Exhaustive Galvanism Self-Experiment | Alexander von Humboldt, Berlin | Applies electrical wires to open blister wounds on his own back and tongue, recording sensations and muscle spasms. | `bci_tech` |
| 339 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1798 | Death of Luigi Galvani: Tragic Pioneer of Neural Physics | Luigi Galvani, Bologna | Galvani dies in poverty after refusing allegiance to Napoleons republic, vindicated posthumously by neuroscience. | `brain_history` |
| 340 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1798 | John Haslam: Early Record of Neurosyphilitic General Paresis | John Haslam, Bethlem Royal Hospital | Documents dementia paralytica with delusions of grandiosity, pupillary abnormalities, and progressive paralysis. | `brain_health` |
| 341 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1799 | Giovanni Fabbroni: Chemical Oxidation vs Pure Bioelectricity | Giovanni Valentino Fabbroni, Florence | Demonstrates electrochemical oxidation accompanies bimetallic contact, advancing understanding of ionic currents. | `bci_tech` |
| 342 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1799 | Humphry Davy: Nitrous Oxide Altering Brain Consciousness | Humphry Davy, Pneumatic Institution | Inhales laughing gas, documenting dissociation, anesthesia, and altered cerebral states, predicting surgical use. | `brain_health` |
| 343 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1800 | Alessandro Volta: The Voltaic Pile Powers Continuous | Alessandro Volta, Royal Society | Invention of chemical battery provides\continuous electrical current, unlocking modern neural stimulation. | `bci_tech` |
| 344 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1800 | Philippe Pinel: Medical-Philosophical Treatise on Insanity | Philippe Pinel, Paris | Formalizes moral therapy and psychological rehabilitation for traumatic brain injury and organic psychosis. | `brain_health` |
| 345 | 4. Enlightenment & Electrical Discovery | 1800 | Xavier Bichat: Organic vs Animal Life Division of Nerves | Xavier Bichat, Paris | Recherches physiologiques separates autonomic vegetative ganglia from somatic conscious cerebral systems. | `brain_basics` |

## 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion (#346 – #545)

| # | Era | Year / Period | Topic Title | Key Figure(s) / Institution | 1-Sentence Core Summary | Pillar |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 346 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1801 | Gall Banished from Vienna for Dangerous Materialism | Franz Joseph Gall, Vienna | Austrian Emperor bans phrenology lectures, ruling brain localization of morality subverts dogma. | `bci_ethics` |
| 347 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1801 | Thomas Young: Trichromatic Three-Receptor Color Theory | Thomas Young, Royal Society London | Proposes human retina needs only three classes of resonant nerve fibers (red, green, violet) to paint all colors. | `brain_basics` |
| 348 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1802 | Jean-Marc Itard: Educating Victor the Wild Boy of Aveyron | Jean-Marc Gaspard Itard, National Deaf Institute Paris | Five-year sensory training of a feral child reveals critical early developmental windows for human language. | `brain_basics` |
| 349 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1803 | Giovanni Aldini: Electrifying Executed Murderers Corpse | Giovanni Aldini, London | Galvanic battery applied to convict corpse causes facial grimaces, inspiring Frankenstein. | `bci_tech` |
| 350 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1804 | Friedrich Serturner: Isolation of Crystalline Morphine | Friedrich Wilhelm Serturner, Paderborn | Purifies Morphium from raw opium poppy latex, creating the first pure alkaloid analgesic targeting central pain. | `brain_health` |
| 351 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1805 | Cuvier: Comparative Neuroanatomy of Brain Weights | Georges Cuvier, Paris | Calculates brain-to-body ratios across vertebrates, linking relative size to cognitive capacity. | `brain_basics` |
| 352 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1807 | Johann Christian Reil: Alcohol Fixation Reveals White Tracts | Johann Christian Reil, Halle | Soaks soft brains in alcohol and alkali to harden tissue, dissecting the corona radiata, tapetum, and insula. | `brain_basics` |
| 353 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1808 | Gall and Spurzheim: Formal Paris Memoir Submission | Gall & Spurzheim | Present anatomical dissections of brain tracts to French Institute; Cuvier and Napoleon reject it. | `brain_history` |
| 354 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1809 | Luigi Rolando: Cerebellar Ablation Destroys Harmony | Luigi Rolando, Sassari | Surgically removes cerebellum in birds, observing ataxia while intellect and vision persist. | `brain_basics` |
| 355 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1811 | Charles Bell: Ventral Spinal Roots Mediate Motor Power | Charles Bell, London | Idea of a New Anatomy of the Brain demonstrates anterior roots stimulate muscle action. | `brain_basics` |
| 356 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1811 | Johann Spurzheim: Popularizing Phrenology Across Europe | Johann Gaspar Spurzheim, Vienna & London | Coins the term phrenology and expands Galls craniological map to 35 localized cortical organs of mind. | `brain_history` |
| 357 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1812 | Cesar Legallois: Locating Medullary Respiratory Center | Julien Legallois, Paris | Pinpoints noeud vital in medulla oblongata whose focal transection arrests automatic breathing. | `brain_basics` |
| 358 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1813 | Thomas Sutton: Delirium Tremens Distinguished from Phrenitis | Thomas Sutton, Kent & London | Identifies acute alcohol withdrawal tremor and visual hallucinations, treating them with sedation over bleeding. | `brain_health` |
| 359 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1814 | Abraham Colles: Intracranial Venous Sinus Rupture | Abraham Colles, Dublin | Clinical observations on transverse sinus lacerations following basilar skull fractures. | `brain_health` |
| 360 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1816 | Karl Friedrich Burdach: Naming Cingulate, Uncus, and Putamen | Karl Friedrich Burdach, Konigsberg | Vom Baue und Leben des Gehirns names the fasciculus cuneatus, lentiform nucleus, claustrum, and amygdala. | `brain_basics` |
| 361 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1817 | James Parkinson: An Essay on the Shaking Palsy | James Parkinson, London | Describes paralysis agitans: resting tremor, rigidity, stooped posture, and festinating gait. | `brain_health` |
| 362 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1818 | Arthur Schopenhauer: Cerebral Primacy of the Will | Arthur Schopenhauer, Dresden | The World as Will and Representation arrives: brain is physical manifestation of mind. | `brain_mysteries` |
| 363 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1819 | John Bostock: Summer Catarrh and Neurogenic Hay Fever | John Bostock, Medical & Chirurgical Society London | Clinically characterizes seasonal paroxysmal sneezing and trigeminal nasal hyper-reflexia in his own nervous system. | `brain_health` |
| 364 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1820 | Hans Christian Oersted: Electromagnetism Unlocks GALVA | Hans Christian Oersted, Copenhagen | Discovery of electromagnetism enables invention of sensitive neural galvanometers. | `bci_tech` |
| 365 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1821 | Charles Bell: Facial Nerve Paralysis (Bells Palsy) | Charles Bell, London | Distinguishes facial nerve motor paralysis from trigeminal sensory neuralgia. | `brain_health` |
| 366 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1822 | Antoine Laurent Bayle: Arachnitis and General Paresis Link | Antoine Laurent Bayle, Charenton Asylum Paris | First clear anatomo-clinical proof linking chronic meningoencephalitis to progressive paralysis and dementia. | `brain_health` |
| 367 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1822 | Francois Magendie: Sensory Dorsal Roots Proven | Francois Magendie, Paris | Severs puppy spinal roots, proving dorsal roots carry sensation and ventral roots command motion. | `brain_basics` |
| 368 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1823 | Pierre Flourens: Equipotentiality of Cerebral Cortex | Marie-Jean-Pierre Flourens, Paris | Ablates cerebral hemispheres in pigeons, asserting cortex acts as a unitary equipotential mass. | `brain_mysteries` |
| 369 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1824 | Flourens: Semicircular Canals Rule Spatial Balance | Pierre Flourens, Paris | Punctures avian semicircular canals, causing violent rolling and loss of spatial orientation. | `brain_basics` |
| 370 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1824 | Leopoldo Nobili: Astatic Galvanometer Measures Frog Currents | Leopoldo Nobili, Modena & Florence | Invents dual-needle astatic galvanometer canceling earths magnetic field to detect micro-currents in frog nerves. | `bci_tech` |
| 371 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1825 | Jan Purkinje: The Purkinje Shift in Scotopic Night Vision | Jan Evangelista Purkinje, Breslau | Observes peak spectral sensitivity shifts from red-yellow in daylight to blue-green in dim twilight rod vision. | `brain_basics` |
| 372 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1825 | Jean-Baptiste Bouillaud: Speech in Anterior Lobes | Jean-Baptiste Bouillaud, Paris | Clinical-autopsy series shows loss of articulate speech always results from frontal lesions. | `brain_health` |
| 373 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1825 | Luigi Rolando: Saggio and the Central Sulcus | Luigi Rolando, Turin | Saggio sopra la vera struttura del cervello maps the Rolandic fissure in human brains. | `brain_basics` |
| 374 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1826 | Johannes Muller: Law of Specific Nerve Energies | Johannes Muller, Univ. of Berlin | Formulates principle that each sensory nerve produces its own unique quality of sensation. | `brain_basics` |
| 375 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1827 | Francois Magendie: Discovery of the Foramen of Magendie | Francois Magendie, College de France Paris | Identifies the median aperture in the fourth ventricle roof allowing CSF to circulate into subarachnoid space. | `brain_basics` |
| 376 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1827 | Richard Bright: Uremic Encephalopathy and Edema | Richard Bright, London | Links renal failure to cerebral edema, coma, and intracranial capillary high pressures. | `brain_health` |
| 377 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1828 | Burke and Hare Murders: Cadaver Trafficking Scandal | Robert Knox, Edinburgh | Serial murders for anatomical dissection cadavers spark Edinburgh Anatomy Act of 1832. | `bci_ethics` |
| 378 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1829 | Charles Bell: Nervous System of the Human Body | Charles Bell, London | Comprehensive monograph unifies cranial nerves with emotional respiratory expressions. | `brain_basics` |
| 379 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1829 | Louis Braille: Six-Dot Tactile Code Recruits Visual Cortex | Louis Braille, National Institute for Blind Youth Paris | Invents raised 6-dot reading system engineered to match the two-point tactile resolution of the human index finger. | `brain_basics` |
| 380 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1830 | Joseph Jackson Lister: Achromatic Lenses End Spherical Blur | Joseph Jackson Lister, London | Solves chromatic aberration in compound microscopes, unlocking sub-micron resolution of nerve cells and axons. | `bci_tech` |
| 381 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1831 | Robert Brown: Identifying the Cellular Nucleus | Robert Brown, Linnean Society | Discovery of cell nucleus provides critical microscopic marker for identifying neuronal somata. | `brain_basics` |
| 382 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1832 | Christian Gottfried Ehrenberg: First Microscopic Nerve Tubes | Christian Gottfried Ehrenberg, Univ. of Berlin | Publishes earliest microscopic observation of varicose nerve tubules inside living vertebrate brain tissue. | `brain_basics` |
| 383 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1832 | Marshall Hall: Reflex Arcs Independent of Brain | Marshall Hall, Royal Society | Establishes spinal cord mediates autonomous reflex actions entirely separate from voluntary brain. | `brain_basics` |
| 384 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1833 | Hall: Three Functional Divisions of the Nervous System | Marshall Hall, London | Classifies cerebral (voluntary), true spinal (excito-motor), and ganglionic (nutritive) networks. | `brain_basics` |
| 385 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1834 | Ernst Weber: Two-Point Tactile Discrimination Gauge | Ernst Heinrich Weber, Leipzig | Uses compass calipers to map cutaneous tactile resolution, revealing dense fingertip innervation. | `brain_basics` |
| 386 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1835 | Adolphe Quetelet: Bell Curve of Human Mental Traits | Adolphe Quetelet, Brussels | Applies statistical normal distribution to human brain capacities and reaction variations. | `brain_basics` |
| 387 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1835 | Filippo Pacini: Discovery of Lamellar Pacinian Corpuscles | Filippo Pacini, Pistoia & Pisa | Identifies onion-like mechanoreceptor capsules in human fingertips that transduce high-frequency vibration. | `brain_basics` |
| 388 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1836 | Gabriel Valentin: First Drawing of Nerve Cell Soma | Gabriel Valentin, Bern | Publishes earliest microscopic illustrations of ganglionic nerve cells with nuclei and axons. | `brain_basics` |
| 389 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1836 | Marc Dax: Unilateral Left Hemisphere Aphasia Link | Marc Dax, Montpellier | Notes all 40 speech-impaired patients presented with left cerebral hemisphere strokes and lesions. | `brain_health` |
| 390 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1837 | Jan Purkinje: Giant Cerebellar Purkinje Cells | Jan Evangelista Purkinje, Prague | Discovers giant flask-shaped neurons in middle cerebellar cortex with massive dendritic trees. | `brain_basics` |
| 391 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1837 | Morse & Wheatstone: Telegraph Networks as Neural Metaphor | Samuel Morse & Charles Wheatstone, London & NY | Electrical telegraph wires transform scientific modeling of white matter tracts into insulated biological cables. | `bci_tech` |
| 392 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1837 | William Sharpey: Ependymal Cilia Propelling CSF | William Sharpey, London | Discovers microscopic oscillating cilia along ependymal lining propelling cerebrospinal fluid. | `brain_basics` |
| 393 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1838 | Charles Wheatstone: Stereoscope Reveals Binocular Disparity | Charles Wheatstone, Kings College London | Mirror stereoscope proves the brain fuses slightly offset left and right retinal images into vivid 3D depth. | `brain_basics` |
| 394 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1838 | Esquirol: Hallucinations Separated from Sensory Illusions | Jean-Etienne Dominique Esquirol, Salpetriere Paris | Des Maladies Mentales draws the definitive clinical boundary between central hallucinations and sensory illusions. | `brain_health` |
| 395 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1838 | Purkinje: Nerve Fibers Originate from Cell Bodies | Jan Purkinje, Breslau | Proves central white matter axons are direct cytoplasmic extensions of gray matter ganglion cells. | `brain_basics` |
| 396 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1838 | Robert Remak: Discovery of Unmyelinated Fibers | Robert Remak, Berlin | Identifies thin, gray, unmyelinated axons (fibers of Remak) composing visceral autonomic networks. | `brain_basics` |
| 397 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1839 | Theodor Schwann: Cell Theory Extends to Sheaths | Theodor Schwann, Leuven | Mikroskopische Untersuchungen discovers Schwann cells forming peripheral myelin sheaths. | `brain_basics` |
| 398 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1840 | Adolph Hannover: Chromic Acid Hardening of Brain Tissue | Adolph Hannover, Copenhagen | Discovers chromic acid preserves delicate neural morphology without shrinkage, enabling thin histological slicing. | `bci_tech` |
| 399 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1840 | Jakob Henle: Sympathetic Vasomotor Smooth Muscle Coats | Friedrich Gustav Jakob Henle, Zurich | Demonstrates arterial walls contain muscular layers innervated by nerves, providing anatomical proof of vasomotion. | `brain_basics` |
| 400 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1840 | Jules Baillarger: Six Laminar Layers of Cortex | Jules Baillarger, Paris | Identifies alternating white and gray stripes (lines of Baillarger) dividing cortex into 6 layers. | `brain_basics` |
| 401 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1841 | James Braid: Coining Hypnotism to Explain Trance | James Braid, Manchester | Demonstrates hypnotic trance stems from neuro-physiological cerebral attention fatigue, not magnetism. | `brain_mysteries` |
| 402 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1842 | Benedikt Stilling: Frozen Serial Sectioning of Brainstem | Benedikt Stilling, Cassel Germany | Freezes spinal cord and brainstem to slice continuous serial cross-sections, mapping cranial nerve nuclei. | `brain_basics` |
| 403 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1842 | Carlo Matteucci: Injury Current in Demarcated Muscle | Carlo Matteucci, Pisa | Records resting electrical injury potential with galvanometer, foundation for action potentials. | `bci_tech` |
| 404 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1842 | Crawford Long: Ether Anesthesia in Surgical Excision | Crawford Long, Georgia | First clinical administration of sulfuric ether to silence surgical pain, left unpublished for years. | `brain_health` |
| 405 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1843 | Emil du Bois-Reymond: Negative Variation in Nerves | Emil du Bois-Reymond, Berlin | Detects transient negative electrical wave passing along stimulated nerve: action potential born! | `bci_tech` |
| 406 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1843 | Maughton Insanity Rule: Forensic Neuro-Responsibility Law | House of Lords, London | Daniel Maughtons paranoid delusion trial establishes legal test for criminal insanity based on cognitive defect. | `bci_ethics` |
| 407 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1844 | Arthur Ladbroke Wigan: The Duality of the Mind | Arthur Ladbroke Wigan, London | Proposes each cerebral hemisphere produces a distinct consciousness capable of internal conflict. | `brain_mysteries` |
| 408 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1844 | Remak: Cardiac Ganglia Regulating Autonomic Beats | Robert Remak, Berlin | Discovery of intrinsic nerve ganglia inside cardiac walls proves local autonomic regulation. | `brain_basics` |
| 409 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1845 | Jacques-Joseph Moreau: Hashish and Mental Illness Model | Jacques-Joseph Moreau de Tours, Club des Hashischins | Administers cannabis extract to study model psychoses, proposing dreams and delirium share neurochemical mechanisms. | `brain_mysteries` |
| 410 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1845 | Weber Brothers: Vagus Nerve Stimulation Halts Heart | Ernst & Eduard Weber, Leipzig | Electrostimulating peripheral vagus nerve stops heartbeats: first proof of neural inhibition! | `brain_basics` |
| 411 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1846 | Rudolf Virchow: Cerebral Embolism Causes Ischemic Stroke | Rudolf Virchow, Charite Berlin | Proves distant cardiac blood clots can travel into middle cerebral arteries to cause acute ischemic softening. | `brain_health` |
| 412 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1846 | William Morton: Ether Dome Public Demonstration | William Morton, Mass General | Public demonstration of ether in Boston: Gentlemen, this is no humbug! Cerebral pain abolished. | `brain_health` |
| 413 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1847 | Carl Ludwig: Kymograph Smoke-Drum Physiological Recorder | Carl Ludwig, Univ. of Marburg | Invents rotating smoked-paper drum kymograph, enabling continuous graphical recording of nerve and vascular waves. | `bci_tech` |
| 414 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1847 | James Simpson: Chloroform Anesthesia Introduced | James Young Simpson, Edinburgh | Introduces potent chloroform vapor for neurosurgical pain relief, validated by Queen Victoria. | `brain_health` |
| 415 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1848 | Emil du Bois-Reymond: Thierische Elektricitat Bible | Emil du Bois-Reymond, Berlin | Untersuchungen uber thierische Elektricitat formulates electrophysiology as rigorous science. | `bci_tech` |
| 416 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1848 | Phineas Gage: Tamping Iron Destroys Frontal Lobe | John Martyn Harlow, Cavendish | Iron rod blasts clean through prefrontal cortex; Gage survives, but his moral soul is gone. | `brain_history` |
| 417 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1849 | Claude Bernard: Piqure Diabetique Brainstem Glucose Control | Claude Bernard, College de France Paris | Puncturing the fourth ventricle floor in rabbits triggers instant glycosuria, proving brain regulates metabolism. | `brain_basics` |
| 418 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1849 | Helmholtz: Measuring Nerve Speed at 27 Meters/sec | Hermann von Helmholtz, Konigsberg | Applies myograph to frog sciatic nerve, proving neural signals travel at measurable 27 m/s! | `brain_basics` |
| 419 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1850 | Augustus Waller: Wallerian Degeneration of Axons | Augustus Volney Waller, Geneva | Sudden disintegration of axons distal to transection proves trophic dependence on somatic body. | `brain_basics` |
| 420 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1850 | Helmholtz: Calculating Sensory Nerve Speed in Humans | Hermann von Helmholtz, Berlin | Uses subtractive reaction times at toe vs thigh to estimate human sensory conduction speeds. | `brain_basics` |
| 421 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1851 | Alfonso Corti: Discovery of the Cochlear Organ | Alfonso Corti, Wurzburg | Identifies microscopic hair cells on basilar membrane transducing sound waves into acoustic spikes. | `brain_basics` |
| 422 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1851 | Heinrich Muller: Radial Glia Cells in the Retina | Heinrich Muller, Wurzburg | Discovers massive radial glial fibers anchoring retinal laminae and supporting photoreceptors. | `brain_basics` |
| 423 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1851 | Helmholtz: Ophthalmoscope Illuminates Living Retina | Hermann von Helmholtz, Berlin | Device reflects light into living eyes, granting first window into central nervous system tissues. | `bci_tech` |
| 424 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1851 | Jean-Pierre Falret: La Folie Circulaire Bipolar Disorder | Jean-Pierre Falret, Salpetriere Paris | Identifies alternating cycles of manic excitation and melancholic depression as a single hereditary brain disease. | `brain_health` |
| 425 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1852 | Brown-Sequard: Hemi-Transection of the Spinal Cord | Charles-Edouard Brown-Sequard | Formulates Brown-Sequard syndrome: ipsilateral motor paralysis with contralateral sensory loss. | `brain_basics` |
| 426 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1852 | Claude Bernard: Sympathetic Vasomotor Neural Control | Claude Bernard, College de France | Severing cervical sympathetic trunk dilates ear vessels, proving neural vasomotor control. | `brain_basics` |
| 427 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1852 | Georg Meissner & Wagner: Tactile Meissner Corpuscles Found | Georg Meissner & Rudolf Wagner, Gottingen | Discover encapsulated low-threshold mechanoreceptors in dermal papillae mediating fine light touch and slip. | `brain_basics` |
| 428 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1852 | Hermann von Helmholtz: Young-Helmholtz Color Theory | Hermann von Helmholtz, Konigsberg | Quantifies spectral wavelength mixing to validate trichromatic cone photoreception in the human retina. | `brain_basics` |
| 429 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1853 | Karl von Vierordt: First Mechanical Sphygmograph | Karl von Vierordt, Tubingen | Invents lever tool to graph arterial pulse waveforms, paving way for cranial perfusion studies. | `bci_tech` |
| 430 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1853 | Moritz Romberg: Lehrbuch and Rombergs Proprioception | Moritz Heinrich Romberg, Berlin | Tabes dorsalis patients sway when eyes close, revealing dorsal column sensory deficits. | `brain_health` |
| 431 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1854 | Bartolomeo Panizza: Occipital Cortex Lesions Cause Blindness | Bartolomeo Panizza, Univ. of Pavia | Experimental posterior cortical ablations and human stroke autopsies localize vision to the occipital lobes. | `brain_basics` |
| 432 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1854 | Louis-Gratiolet: Standardizing the 4 Cerebral Lobes | Louis-Pierre Gratiolet, Paris | Definitively standardizes boundaries of frontal, parietal, temporal, and occipital cerebral lobes. | `brain_basics` |
| 433 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1855 | Albrecht von Graefe: Papilledema Signals Tumor | Albrecht von Graefe, Berlin | Choking of optic disc recognized as primary clinical hallmark of intracranial pressure. | `brain_health` |
| 434 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1855 | Herbert Spencer: Principles of Psychology Evolutionary Mind | Herbert Spencer, London | Argues nervous systems evolve from simple reflex arcs into complex hierarchical cortical associative networks. | `brain_history` |
| 435 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1855 | Richard Caton: Early Primate Electrophysiology | Richard Caton, Edinburgh | Initiates experiments on recording feeble bio-currents from cerebral surfaces in animal models. | `bci_tech` |
| 436 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1856 | Claude Bernard: Curare Paralysis Isolates Neuromuscular Site | Claude Bernard, Paris | Proves Amazonian arrow poison curare blocks transmission between motor nerve and muscle without killing either. | `brain_basics` |
| 437 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1856 | Rudolf Virchow: Cellular Pathology and Neuroglia | Rudolf Virchow, Berlin | Coins Nervenkitt (neuroglia) for interstitial connective cells, extending cell theory. | `brain_basics` |
| 438 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1857 | Benedict Morel: Degeneration Theory Controversy | Benedict Morel, Rouen | Controversial theory claims psychopathology accumulates hereditarily across generations. | `bci_ethics` |
| 439 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1857 | Charles Locock: Potassium Bromide First Antiepileptic Drug | Sir Charles Locock, Royal Medical Society London | Introduces potassium bromide to suppress epileptic convulsions, achieving the first effective pharmacological control. | `brain_health` |
| 440 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1858 | Jerosch von Gerlach: Carmine Staining Uncovers Fibers | Joseph von Gerlach, Erlangen | Accidental carmine dye solution reveals fine dendritic arborizations under compound microscopes. | `brain_basics` |
| 441 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1858 | Virchow-Robin Spaces: Perivascular Fluid Clearance | Virchow & Charles Robin | Describes fluid-padded sleeves surrounding cerebral vessels, anatomical basis for glymphatic flow. | `brain_basics` |
| 442 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1859 | Charles Darwin: Origin of Species Molds Neurology | Charles Darwin, London | Bases neural development on natural selection, aligning human cerebral function with vertebrate roots. | `brain_history` |
| 443 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1859 | Eduard Pfluger: Laws of Electrotonus in Polarized Nerves | Eduard Friedrich Wilhelm Pfluger, Berlin & Bonn | Demonstrates catelectrotonus enhances nerve excitability near the cathode while anelectrotonus suppresses it. | `bci_tech` |
| 444 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1859 | Jean-Baptiste Landry: Acute Ascending Paralysis Syndrome | Jean-Baptiste Octave Landry, Paris | Describes rapid symmetrical paralysis ascending from legs to respiratory nerves (Guillain-Barre precursor). | `brain_health` |
| 445 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1860 | Gustav Fechner: Psychophysics and Logarithmic Scale | Gustav Fechner, Leipzig | Elemente der Psychophysik defines Weber-Fechner law: mental sensation scales with log of stimulus. | `brain_basics` |
| 446 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1861 | Broca: We Speak with the Left Hemisphere | Paul Broca, Paris | Announces hemispheric lateralization: articulate speech is an exclusive function of left cortex. | `brain_basics` |
| 447 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1861 | Paul Broca: Patient Tan Autopsy Localizes Speech | Paul Broca, Bicetre Paris | Leborgnes autopsy reveals softened lesion in left inferior frontal gyrus: Brocas area localized! | `brain_history` |
| 448 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1861 | Prosper Meniere: Inner Ear Labyrinth Causes Vertigo Attacks | Prosper Meniere, Imperial Academy Paris | Overthrows dogma that vertigo is cerebral apoplexy, localizing episodic spinning, tinnitus, and deafness to inner ear. | `brain_health` |
| 449 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1862 | Duchenne de Boulogne: Electrostimulating Facial Nerves | Guillaume Duchenne, Paris | Applies localized galvanic probes to facial nerves, mapping sincere Duchenne smiles vs fake grimaces. | `bci_tech` |
| 450 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1862 | William Little: Birth Asphyxia Causes Spastic Cerebral Palsy | William John Little, Obstetrical Society London | Links neonatal hypoxia and difficult obstetrical delivery to lifelong spastic diplegia (Littles disease). | `brain_health` |
| 451 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1863 | Hermann von Helmholtz: Tonotopic Resonance of Basilar Fibers | Hermann von Helmholtz, Heidelberg | Sensations of Tone models the cochlear basilar membrane as a tuned piano harp performing Fourier frequency analysis. | `brain_basics` |
| 452 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1863 | Ivan Sechenov: Reflexes of the Brain and Inhibition | Ivan Sechenov, St. Petersburg | Applies salt crystals to frog thalamus, discovering brain mechanisms that suppress spinal reflexes. | `brain_basics` |
| 453 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1863 | Nikolaus Friedreich: Hereditary Spinocerebellar Ataxia | Nikolaus Friedreich, Heidelberg | Classifies Friedreichs ataxia: progressive wasting of spinal dorsal columns in adolescence. | `brain_health` |
| 454 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1864 | John Hughlings Jackson: Focal Cortical Seizures | John Hughlings Jackson, London | Decodes Jacksonian march: epilepsy is sudden excessive unbridled discharge of gray matter. | `brain_health` |
| 455 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1865 | Jules Luys: Discovery of the Subthalamic Nucleus (Luysii) | Jules Bernard Luys, Salpetriere Paris | Publishes photographic brain atlas and identifies the subthalamic nucleus (Corpus Luysii), future prime DBS target! | `brain_basics` |
| 456 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1865 | Otto Deiters: Morphological Division of Axons | Otto Deiters, Univ. of Bonn | Posthumous atlas conclusively separates the single axis cylinder from protoplasmic dendrites. | `brain_basics` |
| 457 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1866 | Langdon Down: Characterization of Trisomy 21 | John Langdon Down, Earlswood | First comprehensive clinical description of cognitive differences later recognized as down syndrome. | `brain_health` |
| 458 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1866 | Max Schultze: Duplicity Theory of Retinal Rods and Cones | Max Johann Sigismund Schultze, Univ. of Bonn | Establishes that retinal rods mediate high-sensitivity night vision while cones mediate daytime color acuity. | `brain_basics` |
| 459 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1867 | Theodor Meynert: Cytoarchitectonic Regionalization | Theodor Meynert, Vienna | Divides cortex into projection vs association zones, training Freud, Wernicke, and Flechsig. | `brain_basics` |
| 460 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1868 | FC Donders: Mental Chronometry Measures Decision Speed | Franciscus Donders, Utrecht | Uses reaction-time apparatus to calculate exact milliseconds required for human mental choice. | `brain_basics` |
| 461 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1868 | Jean-Martin Charcot: Multiple Sclerosis Triad | Jean-Martin Charcot, Paris | Correlates Charcots triad (nystagmus, tremor, scanning speech) with demyelinating plaques. | `brain_health` |
| 462 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1868 | Julius Bernstein: Differential Rheotome Tracks Spike Wave | Julius Bernstein, Heidelberg | Invents high-speed rotating contact rheotome to sample the millisecond waveform and conduction velocity of nerve spikes. | `bci_tech` |
| 463 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1869 | Charcot: Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis Described | Jean-Martin Charcot, Salpetriere | Delineates degeneration of lateral corticospinal tracts and anterior horn cells (ALS). | `brain_health` |
| 464 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1869 | Francis Galton: Hereditary Genius and Capacities | Francis Galton, London | Publishes eugenic assertion that intellectual genius is strictly hereditary, sparking ethics gaps. | `bci_ethics` |
| 465 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1869 | Johann Friedrich Horner: Oculosympathetic Palsy Triad | Johann Friedrich Horner, Univ. of Zurich | Demonstrates cervical sympathetic pathway damage causes ipsilateral ptosis, miosis, and facial anhidrosis. | `brain_health` |
| 466 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1870 | Fritsch & Hitzig: Overthrowing Unitarian Cortex | Fritsch & Hitzig, Berlin | Publication shatters 50 years of Flourens dogma, proving specific cortical areas execute motions. | `brain_history` |
| 467 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1870 | Fritsch and Hitzig: Canine Motor Cortex Stimulated | Gustav Fritsch & Eduard Hitzig | Plug electrodes into living dog cortex, provoking focal contralateral limb movements! | `bci_tech` |
| 468 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1871 | Christian Bohr: Gas Exchange in Cerebral Vessels | Christian Bohr, Copenhagen | Investigates oxygen binding kinetics and carbon dioxide displacement in cranial microvasculature. | `brain_basics` |
| 469 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1871 | Louis-Ranvier: Discovery of Nodes of Ranvier | Louis-Antoine Ranvier, Paris | Identifies gated nodal gaps along myelinated axons where saltatory conduction will be decoded. | `brain_basics` |
| 470 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1871 | Silas Weir Mitchell: Coining Phantom Limb and Causalgia | Silas Weir Mitchell, Turner Lane Hospital Philadelphia | Studies Civil War nerve wounds, coining phantom limb and describing burning causalgia from partial nerve injury. | `brain_health` |
| 471 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1872 | Darwin: Expression of the Emotions in Man and Animal | Charles Darwin, London | Proves facial neuromuscular emotional displays are universal across cultures and shared with apes. | `brain_history` |
| 472 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1872 | Gabriel Lippmann: Capillary Electrometer Bioelectric Sensor | Gabriel Lippmann, Sorbonne Paris | Invents mercury-sulfuric acid capillary electrometer whose meniscus shifts with microvolt nerve and heart potentials. | `bci_tech` |
| 473 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1872 | George Huntington: Hereditary Chorea Documented | George Huntington, Long Island | Addresses autosomal dominant chorea, delineating adult onset, motor writhing, and dementia. | `brain_health` |
| 474 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1873 | Camillo Golgi: The Black Reaction Silver Stain | Camillo Golgi, Abbiategrasso | Reazione nera solves histology: silver precipitation stains complete individual neurons. | `brain_history` |
| 475 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1873 | David Ferrier: Faradic Mapping of Primate Cortex | David Ferrier, Yorkshire | Applies precise induction currents to monkey brains, building cerebral functional maps for humans. | `bci_tech` |
| 476 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1874 | Carl Wernicke: Sensory Receptive Aphasia Decoded | Carl Wernicke, Breslau | Der aphasische Symptomencomplex localizes speech comprehension to left superior temporal gyrus. | `brain_history` |
| 477 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1874 | Mary Putnam Jacobi: Defending Female Cerebral Powers | Mary Putnam Jacobi, Harvard | Publishes physiological refutation of assertion that higher study atrophies female brains. | `brain_history` |
| 478 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1874 | Roberts Bartholow: First Faradic Stimulation of Human Brain | Roberts Bartholow, Medical College of Ohio | Inserts insulated needle electrodes into exposed cortex of patient Mary Rafferty, evoking contralateral limb twitches. | `bci_ethics` |
| 479 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1874 | Vladimir Betz: Giant Pyramidal Cells in Layer V | Vladimir Betz, Univ. of Kiev | Discovers massive projection neurons (Betz cells) forming great corticospinal highways. | `brain_basics` |
| 480 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1875 | Friedrich Merkel: Discovery of Epidermal Merkel Touch Discs | Friedrich Sigmund Merkel, Univ. of Rostock | Identifies Tastzellen (Merkel cell-neurite complexes) in basal epidermis encoding sustained pressure and texture. | `brain_basics` |
| 481 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1875 | Richard Caton: Recording Electrical Brain Waves | Richard Caton, Liverpool | Places galvanometer electrodes on living animal cortex: first ever recording of EEG bioelectricity! | `bci_tech` |
| 482 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1875 | Wilhelm Erb and Carl Westphal: The Knee-Jerk Reflex | Wilhelm Erb & Carl Westphal | Patellar tendon percussion evokes involuntary quadriceps jerk, establishing clinical reflex exams. | `brain_basics` |
| 483 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1876 | Cesare Lombroso: The Criminal Skull Measurement Fallacy | Cesare Lombroso, Turin | L Uomo Delinquente claims cranial asymmetries mark born criminals, sparking ethics backlash. | `bci_ethics` |
| 484 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1876 | David Ferrier: Functions of the Brain Guides Scalpel | David Ferrier, Kings College | Comprehensive monograph compiles cortical maps, showing brain tumors can be localized by signs. | `brain_history` |
| 485 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1876 | Franz Boll: Discovery of Photobleaching Visual Purple | Franz Christian Boll, Univ. of Rome | Discovers frog retinas look bright red-purple in darkness and bleach yellow-white upon exposure to light (rhodopsin). | `brain_basics` |
| 486 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1877 | Adolf Kussmaul: Word Deafness and Reading Centers | Adolf Kussmaul, Strasbourg | Die Storungen der Sprache identifies pure word blindness (alexia) as isolated disconnection. | `brain_health` |
| 487 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1877 | Jean-Martin Charcot: Grand Hysteria in Paris | Jean-Martin Charcot, Salpetriere | Staged public demonstrations of hypnotized women generate intense scientific and ethical debate. | `bci_ethics` |
| 488 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1878 | Claude Bernard: Lecons Fixed Internal Milieu Concept | Claude Bernard, College de France Paris | Declares stability of the internal environment (milieu interieur) is the prerequisite for free higher brain life. | `brain_basics` |
| 489 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1878 | Paul Broca: Le Grand Lobe Limbique Defined | Paul Broca, Paris | Identifies ancient ring of cortex around brainstem (limbic lobe), later named emotional brain. | `brain_basics` |
| 490 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1878 | Willy Kuhne: Optography Captures Retinal Rhodopsin Image | Wilhelm Willy Kuhne, Univ. of Heidelberg | Isolates rhodopsin protein and fixes a bleached window pattern onto a rabbit retina like a biological camera film. | `brain_basics` |
| 491 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1879 | Wilhelm Wundt: First Experimental Psychology Lab | Wilhelm Wundt, Univ. of Leipzig | Founds Institute for Experimental Psychology, applying physiological tools to conscious states. | `brain_history` |
| 492 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1879 | William Macewen: First Tumor Excision via Foci | William Macewen, Glasgow | Resects meningioma without trepanation guide, locating lesion solely by contralateral spasms. | `brain_health` |
| 493 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1880 | Jean-Baptiste Gelineau: Coining Narcolepsy and Cataplexy | Jean-Baptiste Edouard Gelineau, Paris | Describes irresistible daytime sleep attacks paired with sudden emotion-triggered muscle tone loss (astasia). | `brain_health` |
| 494 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1880 | Paul Flechsig: Myelogenetic Mapping of Tracts | Paul Flechsig, Leipzig | Maps embryonic myelination: sensory motor fibers myelinate early while association zones lag. | `brain_basics` |
| 495 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1881 | Hermann Munk: Psychic Blindness (Visual Agnosia) in Dogs | Hermann Munk, Berlin Veterinary School | Ablates extrastriate occipital cortex; dogs avoid obstacles by sight yet fail to recognize food or their master. | `brain_basics` |
| 496 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1881 | London Medical Congress: Ferrier vs Goltz Debate | David Ferrier & Friedrich Goltz | Goltz brings decerebrated dog; Ferrier presents hemiplegic monkey; autopsy proves localization! | `brain_history` |
| 497 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1881 | William Gowers: Epilepsy and Convulsive Diseases | William Richard Gowers, London | Definitive clinical treatise analyzing 1,450 seizure cases, detailing aura patterns and care. | `brain_health` |
| 498 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1882 | Charcot: Worlds First Clinical Neurology Chair | Jean-Martin Charcot, Paris | French government creates first university chair of nervous system diseases for Charcot. | `brain_history` |
| 499 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1882 | Magnus Blix & Goldscheider: Cutaneous Warm, Cold, Pain Spots | Magnus Blix, Uppsala & Alfred Goldscheider, Berlin | Map human skin with fine thermal and mechanical probes, proving discrete mosaic spots for cold, warmth, and pain. | `brain_basics` |
| 500 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1883 | Emil Kraepelin: Compendium der Psychiatrie | Emil Kraepelin, Heidelberg | Classifies mental illnesses empirically, laying diagnostic groundwork for biological psychiatry. | `brain_health` |
| 501 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1883 | Max Bruch: Histology of the Tapetum Lucidum | Max Bruch, Wurzburg | Comparative anatomical studies map reflective retinal structures enhancing animal night vision. | `brain_basics` |
| 502 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1884 | Carl Koller: Cocaine Local Anesthesia Breakthrough | Carl Koller & Sigmund Freud | Instills cocaine drops into eye, discovering complete local anesthesia, adopted in neurosurgery. | `brain_health` |
| 503 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1884 | Gilles de la Tourette: Maladie des Tics Documented | Georges Gilles de la Tourette | Charcots student catalogs 9 patients with involuntary motor tics, barking, and coprolalia. | `brain_health` |
| 504 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1884 | Rickman Godlee: Primary Glioma Excision Landmark | Rickman Godlee & Hughes Bennett | Bennett localizes glioma from seizures; Godlee excises it; establishes surgical asepsis need. | `brain_health` |
| 505 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1885 | Hermann Ebbinghaus: Memory Forgetting Curve | Hermann Ebbinghaus, Berlin | Uses nonsense syllables on himself, plotting the universal exponential forgetting curve. | `brain_basics` |
| 506 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1885 | Ludwig Edinger: Comparative Evolution of Vertebrate Brains | Ludwig Edinger, Frankfurt am Main | Distinguishes ancient paleencephalon from mammalian neocortex and identifies the oculomotor Edinger-Westphal nucleus. | `brain_basics` |
| 507 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1885 | Paul Ehrlich: Discovery of Blood-Brain Barrier | Paul Ehrlich, Berlin | Intravenous dyes stain all peripheral tissues except brain, demonstrating selective barrier. | `brain_basics` |
| 508 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1886 | Gowers: Manual of Diseases of the Nervous System | William Richard Gowers, London | Publishes monumental diagnostic neurology text, the undisputed bible of Victorian neurology. | `brain_history` |
| 509 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1886 | Horsley: Awake Cortical Stimulation for Epilepsy | Victor Horsley, London | Electrically stimulates motor cortex in awake epileptic patients to localize seizure focus. | `bci_tech` |
| 510 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1886 | Victor Horsley: Appointed First Neurosurgeon | Victor Horsley, Queen Square | Appointed surgeon to National Hospital for Paralysis and Epilepsy, standardizing craniotomy. | `brain_health` |
| 511 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1887 | Augustus Waller: Human Electrocardiogram Recording | Augustus Waller, London | Connects limbs to capillary electrometer, recording bioelectric signals, advancing neurotech. | `bci_tech` |
| 512 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1887 | Santiago Ramon y Cajal: First Seeing Golgi Stain | Santiago Ramon y Cajal, Madrid | Luis Simarro shows Cajal silver-stained slides; Cajal realizes method can solve neural riddle. | `brain_history` |
| 513 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1887 | Sergei Korsakoff: Amnestic Confabulation in Polyneuritis | Sergei Korsakoff, Moscow Imperial Univ. | Describes alcoholic anterograde amnesia with vivid confabulation (Korsakoff syndrome) from mammillary-thalamic damage. | `brain_health` |
| 514 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1888 | Cajal: Avian Cerebellum Proves Neuron Independence | Santiago Ramon y Cajal, Barcelona | Golgi staining reveals dendritic endings touch without fusing: reticular network theory falls! | `brain_history` |
| 515 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1888 | Cajal: Formulating Law of Dynamic Polarization | Santiago Ramon y Cajal, Barcelona | Postulates neural impulses travel directionally: received at dendrites, discharged along axon. | `brain_basics` |
| 516 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1889 | Angelo Mosso: The Human Circulation Balance Machine | Angelo Mosso, Univ. of Turin | Places human on delicate balance table; intellectual thought shifts blood to head, tilting table! | `brain_basics` |
| 517 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1889 | Cajal: Berlin Anatomy Congress Convinces Masters | Santiago Ramon y Cajal, Berlin | Demonstrates slides to Albert von Kolliker; German anatomy giants adopt Cajals neuron model. | `brain_history` |
| 518 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1889 | Wernicke-Mann Posture & Wernickes Encephalopathy Triad | Carl Wernicke, Breslau | Identifies acute hemorrhagic polioencephalitis superior (confusion, ataxia, ophthalmoplegia) in thiamine deficiency. | `brain_health` |
| 519 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1890 | Adolf Beck: Evoked Cortical Potentials and Desynchronization | Adolf Beck, Jagiellonian Univ. Krakow | Records occipital electrical waves in dogs and rabbits, discovering sensory light flashes block spontaneous rhythms! | `bci_tech` |
| 520 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1890 | Roy and Sherrington: Neurovascular Coupling in the Brain | Charles Roy & Charles Sherrington, Cambridge | Prove local cerebral blood flow automatically dilates in response to regional neuronal metabolic activity—fMRI basis! | `brain_basics` |
| 521 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1890 | Victor Horsley: Thyroid Grafting Reverses Deficits | Victor Horsley, Brown Institution | Demonstrates sheep thyroid grafts reverse surgical cretinism and cognitive collapse in monkeys. | `brain_health` |
| 522 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1890 | William James: Principles of Psychology and Emotion | William James, Harvard | James-Lange theory: bodily autonomic arousal precedes conscious emotion (we are sad because we cry). | `brain_history` |
| 523 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1891 | Franz Nissl: Staining Reveals Somatic Nissl Bodies | Franz Nissl, Heidelberg | Selective basic dye illuminates rough endoplasmic reticulum (Nissl bodies) in neuronal somata. | `brain_basics` |
| 524 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1891 | Heinrich Quincke: Clinical Lumbar Puncture Invented | Heinrich Quincke, Kiel | Introduces hollow needle puncture between lumbar vertebrae to withdraw CSF for diagnosis. | `brain_health` |
| 525 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1891 | Sigmund Freud: On Aphasia Coins the Term Agnosia | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Monograph critiques strict localizationism and coins agnosia for cortical inability to recognize sensory objects. | `brain_basics` |
| 526 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1891 | Wilhelm Waldeyer: Formalizing Term Neuron | Wilhelm von Waldeyer-Hartz | Publishes review unifying Cajal and His work, coining Neuron and formalizing the Neuron Doctrine. | `brain_basics` |
| 527 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1892 | Arnold Pick: Focal Frontotemporal Lobar Atrophy | Arnold Pick, Prague | Autopsies describe progressive language aphasia and behavioral changes linked to temporal atrophy. | `brain_health` |
| 528 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1892 | Joseph Babinski: The Great Toe Extensor Reflex | Joseph Babinski, Paris | Demonstrates stroking sole of foot in pyramidal lesion causes great toe dorsiflexion (Babinski sign). | `brain_health` |
| 529 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1892 | Jules Dejerine: Pure Alexia Without Agraphia Disconnection | Jules Dejerine, Bicetre Paris | Autopsy shows left occipital and corpus callosum splenium stroke leaves patient able to write but blind to own words. | `brain_health` |
| 530 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1893 | Cajal: Discovery of the Axonal Growth Cone | Santiago Ramon y Cajal, Madrid | Identifies amoeboid motile tip (cono de crecimiento) guiding developing axons through tissue. | `brain_basics` |
| 531 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1894 | Cajal: Croonian Lecture on Fine Structure of Nervous System | Santiago Ramon y Cajal, Royal Society London | Delivers the landmark Royal Society Croonian Lecture synthesizing cortical circuits and dynamic polarization. | `brain_history` |
| 532 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1894 | Eugene Devic: Neuromyelitis Optica (Devics Disease) | Eugene Devic & Fernand Gault, Lyon | Delineates acute bilateral optic neuritis combined with transverse myelitis as a distinct demyelinating syndrome. | `brain_health` |
| 533 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1894 | Wilhelm His: Embryology Confirms Single Outgrowth | Wilhelm His, Univ. of Leipzig | Tracks embryonic neuroblasts, proving every nerve fiber grows outward as continuous sprout. | `brain_basics` |
| 534 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1895 | Freud and Breuer: Studies on Hysteria Catharsis | Sigmund Freud & Josef Breuer | Case of Anna O: talking cure reveals repressed emotional trauma converted into paralysis. | `brain_health` |
| 535 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1895 | Wilhelm Rontgen: X-Rays Reveal Living Cranium Bones | Wilhelm Conrad Rontgen | Discovery of penetrative rays enables imaging of skull bone fractures and foreign bullets. | `bci_tech` |
| 536 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1896 | George Stratton: Upside-Down Prism Goggles Visual Adaptation | George Malcolm Stratton, Univ. of California Berkeley | Wears inverting lenses for 8 days; by day 5 his visual cortex adapts and perceives the world upright again! | `brain_basics` |
| 537 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1896 | Horsley and Clarke: Stereotactic Frame Blueprint | Horsley & Clarke, London | Design 3D Cartesian frame to guide needle electrodes to deep cerebellar nuclei with precision. | `bci_tech` |
| 538 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1896 | Kraepelin: Splitting Schizophrenia from Affective | Emil Kraepelin, Heidelberg | Establishes landmark dichotomy between degenerative dementia praecox and episodic affective disorder. | `brain_health` |
| 539 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1897 | Charles Sherrington: Coining the Term Synapse | Charles Scott Sherrington | Fosters Physiology introduces synapse for functional junction where one neuron meets another. | `brain_basics` |
| 540 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1897 | Felix Marchand: Glial Scarring in Brain Damage | Felix Marchand, Leipzig | Describes microglial and astrocytic proliferation forming glial scars that block axonal regrowth. | `brain_health` |
| 541 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1897 | Ivan Pavlov: Digestive Secretions in Dogs | Ivan Pavlov, St. Petersburg | Measures gastric juice flow triggered by footsteps of feeder: psychic secretions found. | `brain_basics` |
| 542 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1897 | Sherrington: Decerebrate Rigidity and Inhibition | Charles Sherrington, Liverpool | Transection between colliculi evokes extensor rigidity, revealing reciprocal motor inhibition. | `brain_basics` |
| 543 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1898 | Camillo Golgi: Discovery of Intracellular Golgi Apparatus | Camillo Golgi, Univ. of Pavia | Uses osmium-dichromate silver staining on owl Purkinje neurons to reveal the internal reticular Golgi organelle. | `brain_basics` |
| 544 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1898 | Edward Thorndike: Puzzle Boxes and the Law of Effect | Edward Lee Thorndike, Columbia Univ. | Cats escaping latch boxes prove behaviors followed by satisfying rewards stamp in neural stimulus-response bonds. | `brain_basics` |
| 545 | 5. 19th Century Neuroscience Explosion | 1899 | Francis Gotch: Refractory Period of Mammalian Nerve Fibers | Francis Gotch, Univ. of Oxford | Demonstrates an absolute refractory millisecond window after an action potential during which no second spike fires. | `brain_basics` |

## 6. Early 20th Century (#546 – #720)

| # | Era | Year / Period | Topic Title | Key Figure(s) / Institution | 1-Sentence Core Summary | Pillar |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 546 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1901 | Alois Alzheimer: Clinical Interview of Auguste Deter | Alois Alzheimer, Frankfurt | Interviews 51-year-old Auguste Deter with rapid memory loss and hallucinations: presenile dementia. | `brain_health` |
| 547 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1901 | Sherrington & Grunbaum: Chimpanzee Motor Cortex Map | Charles Sherrington & Albert Grunbaum, Liverpool | Unipolar faradic mapping in great apes proves primary motor cortex lies strictly anterior to the central sulcus. | `brain_basics` |
| 548 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1901 | Takamine: Adrenaline Purified as First Isolated Hormone | Jokichi Takamine, New York | Isolates and purifies crystalline adrenaline from adrenal medulla, identifying sympathetic neurohormone. | `brain_basics` |
| 549 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1902 | Ernest Overton: Sodium Ions Required for Nerve Excitability | Charles Ernest Overton, Univ. of Wurzburg | Discovers muscles and nerves lose all electrical excitability in sodium-free solutions, predicting Na+ influx. | `brain_basics` |
| 550 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1902 | Harvey Cushing: Continuous Blood Pressure in Cranial Surgery | Harvey Cushing, Johns Hopkins | Introduces Riva-Rocci sphygmomanometer into operating room, dramatically reducing craniotomy deaths. | `brain_health` |
| 551 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1902 | Julius Bernstein: Membrane Hypothesis of Action Potential | Julius Bernstein, Halle | Hypothesizes resting potential is potassium diffusion potential and action potential is breakdown of membrane. | `brain_basics` |
| 552 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1903 | Fischer & von Mering: Synthesis of Barbital Sedative Drug | Emil Fischer & Josef von Mering, Berlin | Synthesize diethylbarbituric acid (Veronal), launching barbiturate neuro-sedation and anesthesia. | `brain_health` |
| 553 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1903 | Ivan Pavlov: Formalization of Conditional Reflex Laws | Ivan Pavlov, Madrid Congress | Delivers address on experimental psychopathology and conditioned salivation reflex mechanisms. | `brain_basics` |
| 554 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1903 | Willem Einthoven: String Galvanometer Records | Willem Einthoven, Leiden | Invents ultra-sensitive string galvanometer, enabling precision recording of bioelectric signals. | `bci_tech` |
| 555 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1904 | Ivan Pavlov: Awarded Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine | Ivan Pavlov, Nobel Committee | Awarded Nobel Prize for physiology of digestion, expanding into conditional cortical reflexes. | `brain_history` |
| 556 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1904 | Thomas Elliott: Sympathetic Adrenaline Chemical Transmission | Thomas Renton Elliott, Cambridge Univ. | Proposes sympathetic nerve impulses act by liberating minute amounts of adrenaline onto effector smooth muscle. | `brain_basics` |
| 557 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1905 | Alfred W. Campbell: Histological Localization of Function | Alfred Walter Campbell, Rainhill Asylum Liverpool | Publishes pioneering human cytoarchitectonic and myeloarchitectonic map dividing cortex into functional fields. | `brain_basics` |
| 558 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1905 | Binet and Simon: First Standardized Metric Scale of Mind | Alfred Binet & Theodore Simon | Designs practical diagnostic mental age battery to identify children needing special education. | `brain_history` |
| 559 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1905 | John Langley: Concept of Receptive Substances in Junctions | John Newport Langley, Cambridge | Curare and nicotine studies lead to foundational concept that cells possess specific receptor sites. | `brain_basics` |
| 560 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1906 | Alzheimer: Neurofibrillary Tangles and Amyloid Plaques | Alois Alzheimer, Tubingen | Uses Bielschowsky silver stain on Deters cortex, discovering intracellular tangles and senile plaques. | `brain_health` |
| 561 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1906 | Dejerine and Roussy: Thalamic Pain Syndrome Described | Jules Dejerine & Gustave Roussy, Salpetriere Paris | Identify posterolateral thalamic stroke causing contralateral hemianesthesia and searing central neuropathic pain. | `brain_health` |
| 562 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1906 | Golgi and Cajal: Bitter Nobel Prize in Physiology Clash | Camillo Golgi & Santiago Ramon y Cajal | Share Nobel Prize; Golgi defends reticular syncytium while Cajal proves neuron doctrine with slides. | `brain_history` |
| 563 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1906 | Pierre Marie: Iconoclastic Revision of Brocas Aphasia | Pierre Marie, Bicetre Paris | Re-examines Leborgnes preserved brain in Paris, arguing subcortical lenticular zone damage drives motor aphasia. | `brain_history` |
| 564 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1906 | Sherrington: Integrative Action of the Nervous System | Charles Scott Sherrington, Yale | Silliman lectures define reflex arcs, synaptic delay, temporal summation, and reciprocal inhibition. | `brain_basics` |
| 565 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1907 | Keith Lucas: All-or-None Law in Single Skeletal Nerve-Muscle | Keith Lucas, Trinity College Cambridge | Demonstrates individual stimulated nerve-muscle units respond maximally or not at all regardless of stimulus strength. | `brain_basics` |
| 566 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1907 | Louis Lapicque: Integrate-and-Fire Model and Chronaxie | Louis Lapicque, Paris | Formulates mathematical integrate-and-fire equation of nerve excitation, defining chronaxie and rheobase. | `brain_basics` |
| 567 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1907 | Ross Harrison: Hanging Drop Culture of Living Nerve Fibers | Ross Granville Harrison, Johns Hopkins | Grows embryonic frog spinal cord in clotted lymph: axon growth cone seen extending in vitro! | `brain_basics` |
| 568 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1908 | Horsley and Clarke: Stereotactic Apparatus in Animals | Victor Horsley & Robert Clarke | Brain paper publishes Cartesian 3D micro-manipulator frame for subcortical electrode placement. | `bci_tech` |
| 569 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1908 | Hugo Liepmann: Left Parietal Dominance for Motor Praxis | Hugo Karl Liepmann, Dalldorf Asylum Berlin | Defines ideomotor and callosal apraxia, proving the left hemisphere plans purposeful tool actions for both hands. | `brain_health` |
| 570 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1908 | Yerkes and Dodson: The Inverted-U Arousal-Performance Law | Robert Yerkes & John Dodson | Demonstrates performance increases with physiological arousal only up to an optimum point before collapsing. | `brain_basics` |

| 572 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1909 | Karplus and Kreidl: Hypothalamic Autonomic Center Stimulated | Johann Paul Karplus & Alois Kreidl, Vienna | Electrical stimulation of the hypothalamus evokes pupillary dilation, sweating, and blood pressure surges. | `brain_basics` |

| 574 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1910 | Henry Dale: Autonomic Effects of Histamine and Ergot | Henry Hallett Dale, Wellcome Lab | Isolates autonomic bioactive amines, identifying differential receptor stimulation across tissues. | `brain_basics` |
| 575 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1910 | Paul Ehrlich: Salvarsan 606 Cures Neurosyphilis Microbe | Paul Ehrlich & Sahachiro Hata | Synthesizes organoarsenic antimicrobial Salvarsan, the first targeted magic bullet for Treponema pallidum. | `brain_health` |
| 576 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1911 | Allvar Gullstrand: Nobel Prize for Optical Dioptrics | Allvar Gullstrand, Nobel Committee | Awarded Nobel Prize for mathematical dioptrics of eye, advancing retinal imaging and ophthalmic optics. | `brain_basics` |
| 577 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1911 | Edouard Claparede: Amnesic Pinprick Proves Implicit Memory | Edouard Claparede, Univ. of Geneva | Hides a pin in his hand while greeting a Korsakoff patient; she forgets him next day yet refuses to shake hands! | `brain_basics` |
| 578 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1911 | Eugen Bleuler: Coining Schizophrenia to Replace Dementia | Eugen Bleuler, Burgholzli Zurich | Replaces Kraepelins dementia praecox with Schizophrenia, emphasizing 4 As: association, affect, autism, ambivalence. | `brain_health` |
| 579 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1912 | Alfred Hauptmann: Phenobarbital Discovered for Epilepsy | Alfred Hauptmann, Freiburg Clinic | Gives Luminal (phenobarbital) to sedate epileptic patients at night, discovering potent seizure suppression! | `brain_health` |
| 580 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1912 | Cushing: The Pituitary Body and Its Disorders Landmark | Harvey Cushing, Harvard | Monograph correlates pituitary hyper- and hyposecretion with acromegaly, gigantism, and sexual infantilism. | `brain_health` |
| 581 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1912 | Max Wertheimer: Phi Phenomenon and Gestalt Movement | Max Wertheimer, Frankfurt | Stroboscopic apparent motion proves brain constructs holistic perceptual wholes not reducible to sensory parts. | `brain_basics` |
| 582 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1912 | Samuel Kinnier Wilson: Hepatolenticular Copper Degeneration | Samuel Alexander Kinnier Wilson, Queen Square London | Monograph describes familial lenticular nucleus degeneration with liver cirrhosis (Wilsons disease) and dystonia. | `brain_health` |
| 583 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1912 | Vladimir Pravdich-Neminsky: First Photographic Canine EEG | Vladimir Pravdich-Neminsky, Kiev Univ. | Uses Einthovens string galvanometer to photograph non-invasive Elektrocerebrogramm waves through an intact dog skull. | `bci_tech` |
| 584 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1913 | Edwin Goldmann: Trypan Blue Directly Injected into CSF | Edwin Ellen Goldmann, Freiburg | Injects dye directly into cerebrospinal fluid, staining the brain blue while body tissues stay clear—proving BBB! | `brain_basics` |
| 585 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1913 | Hideyo Noguchi: Spirochetes Found in Paretic Syphilis Brains | Hideyo Noguchi & J.W. Moore, Rockefeller Institute | Identifies living syphilitic spirochetes inside paretic cerebral cortex, proving general paresis is an infection. | `brain_health` |
| 586 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1913 | John B. Watson: The Behaviorist Manifesto in Psychology | John Broadus Watson, Columbia | Rejects introspective consciousness; demands neuroscience and psychology focus strictly on observable responses. | `brain_history` |
| 587 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1913 | Victor Babes: Rabies Negri Bodies and Viral Neurotropy | Victor Babes, Bucharest | Autopsies demonstrate rabies virus travels along peripheral axoplasm to destroy central hippocampal neurons. | `brain_health` |
| 588 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1914 | Cajal: Degeneration and Regeneration of the Nervous System | Santiago Ramon y Cajal, Madrid | Two-volume study examines axonal sprouting failure in CNS vs Schwann-guided regeneration in peripheral nerves. | `brain_basics` |
| 589 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1914 | Henry Dale: Acetylcholine as Neurotransmitter Candidate | Henry Dale, London | Identifies acetylcholine mimics parasympathetic stimulation, dividing actions into muscarinic and nicotinic. | `brain_basics` |
| 590 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1914 | Robert Barany: Nobel Prize for Vestibular Apparatus | Robert Barany, Nobel Committee | Awarded Nobel Prize for caloric testing and vestibular inner ear reflex mechanisms linked to cerebellar ataxia. | `brain_basics` |
| 591 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1915 | James G. Dusser de Barenne: Strychnine Neuronography Mapping | J.G. Dusser de Barenne, Utrecht & Yale | Applies strychnine-soaked filter paper to cortex to evoke synchronized spikes along connected cortico-cortical tracts. | `bci_tech` |
| 592 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1915 | WWI Cranial Trauma: High-Velocity Bullet Brain Lesions | Military Neurosurgeons, Western Front | Trench warfare sniper wounds provide unprecedented living models of focal cerebral cortical ablations. | `brain_health` |
| 593 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1915 | Walter Cannon: Bodily Changes in Pain, Hunger, Fear, Rage | Walter Cannon, Harvard | Establishes the emergency sympatho-adrenal system preparing the organism for fight or flight. | `brain_basics` |
| 594 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1916 | Gordon Holmes: Retinotopic Map of Human Calcarine Cortex | Gordon Holmes, British Red Cross | Examines visual field scotomas in bullet-wounded soldiers, mapping inverted visual field on occipital poles. | `brain_basics` |
| 595 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1916 | Guillain, Barre & Strohl: Albuminocytological Dissociation | Georges Guillain, Jean-Alexandre Barre & Andre Strohl | Identify high CSF protein with normal cell count in French soldiers with reversible acute radiculoneuropathy. | `brain_health` |
| 596 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1917 | Julius Wagner-Jauregg: Malarial Inoculation for Syphilis | Julius Wagner-Jauregg, Vienna | Inoculates neurosyphilis paretics with tertian malaria; high fevers halt Treponema, inducing remission. | `brain_health` |
| 597 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1917 | Walther Poppelreuter: Visual Agnosia in WWI Brain Trauma | Walther Poppelreuter, Cologne Military Hospital | Develops overlapping-figure tests for occipital gunshot veterans, founding modern clinical visual neuropsychology. | `brain_health` |
| 598 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1917 | Wolfgang Kohler: The Mentality of Apes and Insight Learning | Wolfgang Kohler, Tenerife | Sultan the chimpanzee stacks boxes to reach bananas, proving insightful cognitive problem solving in primates. | `brain_basics` |
| 599 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1918 | Arthur Keith: Piltdown Man Skull Brain Capacity Fraud | Arthur Keith, British Museum | Celebrated missing link skull with human braincase is later exposed as an intentional forgery. | `bci_ethics` |

| 601 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1919 | Konstantin Tretiakoff: Substantia Nigra Loss in Parkinsons | Konstantin Nikolaevich Tretiakoff, Salpetriere Paris | Doctoral thesis proves Parkinsonian rigidity and tremor correlate with dark neuromelanin cell loss in substantia nigra. | `brain_health` |
| 602 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1919 | Pio del Rio-Hortega: Discovery of Microglia and Oligodendria | Pio del Rio-Hortega, Madrid | Uses silver carbonate staining to resolve mesodermal phagocytic microglia and myelin-forming oligodendrocytes. | `brain_basics` |
| 603 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1919 | Walter Dandy: Pneumoencephalography via Lumbar Puncture | Walter Dandy, Baltimore | Introduces lumbar air replacement to outline subarachnoid cisterns and brain sulci on radiographic film. | `bci_tech` |

| 606 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1920 | Henry Head: Studies in Neurology and Protopathic Sensation | Henry Head, London | Severs radial nerve in his own arm, charting return of crude protopathic vs fine epicritic sensation. | `brain_basics` |
| 607 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1920 | Little Albert Experiment: Conditioned Emotional Trauma | Watson & Rosalie Rayner, Johns Hopkins | Unethical conditioning pairs white rat with loud steel bar clang, inducing phobia in 9-month-old infant. | `bci_ethics` |
| 608 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1921 | Hermann Rorschach: Psychodiagnostik Inkblot Perception | Hermann Rorschach, Bern | Develops 10 standardized inkblot plates to diagnose schizophrenia and unconscious perceptual projection. | `brain_mysteries` |
| 609 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1921 | Otto Loewi: Vagusstoff Proves Chemical Transmission | Otto Loewi, Univ. of Graz | Dream inspires midnight experiment: fluid from stimulated frog heart slows second heart: chemical transmission! | `brain_history` |
| 610 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1921 | Russell Wilder: Ketogenic Diet for Refractory Epilepsy | Russell Morse Wilder, Mayo Clinic Rochester | Formulates high-fat, ultra-low-carbohydrate ketogenic regimen to mimic fasting ketosis and halt refractory seizures. | `brain_health` |
| 611 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1922 | Erlanger and Gasser: Cathode Ray Oscilloscope in Nerves | Joseph Erlanger & Herbert Gasser | Couple vacuum tube amplifiers to cathode ray tubes, recording compound action potentials of A, B, C fibers. | `bci_tech` |
| 612 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1922 | Spatz & Hallervorden: Basal Ganglia Iron and Dark Legacy | Hugo Spatz & Julius Hallervorden, Munich | Identify pallidal iron accumulation in neurodegeneration; later tied to unethical Nazi euthanasia brain harvesting. | `bci_ethics` |
| 613 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1922 | Walter Dandy: Total Resection of Acoustic Neuroma | Walter Dandy, Baltimore | First surgical series achieving complete acoustic neuroma excision with facial nerve anatomical preservation. | `brain_health` |
| 614 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1923 | Jean Piaget: The Language and Thought of the Child | Jean Piaget, Geneva | Pioneers clinical interview method, documenting developmental stages of juvenile egocentric reasoning. | `brain_basics` |
| 615 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1923 | Joseph Capgras: The Illusion of Doubles (Capgras Delusion) | Joseph Capgras & Jean Reboul-Lachaux, Paris | Madame M insists her husband and daughter were replaced by identical impostors due to visual-limbic disconnection. | `brain_mysteries` |
| 616 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1923 | Wilder Penfield: Apprenticeship with Sherrington at Oxford | Wilder Penfield, Oxford | Studies motor coordination and histology with Sherrington, dreaming of combining surgery with cortical mapping. | `brain_history` |
| 617 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1924 | Hans Berger: First Recording of Human EEG (Alpha Wave) | Hans Berger, Univ. of Jena | Inserts platinum needles into son Klaus scalp: records rhythmic 10 Hz electrical oscillations (Berger rhythm). | `bci_tech` |

| 624 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1926 | Walter Cannon: Homeostasis and Sympathetic Adrenal Drive | Walter Cannon, Harvard Medical | Coins homeostasis and fight-or-flight response, modeling autonomic emergency responses in brainstem. | `brain_basics` |

| 637 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1930 | Lev Vygotsky: Cultural-Historical Psychology and Brain | Lev Vygotsky, Moscow | Argues higher cortical functions develop through internalization of socio-cultural linguistic tools. | `brain_basics` |

| 640 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1931 | Kurt Goldstein: The Organism Holism in Brain Pathology | Kurt Goldstein, Frankfurt | Examines brain-injured WWI veterans, showing nervous system reorganizes holistically after focal damage. | `brain_health` |

| 646 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1933 | Detlev Bronk: Recording Autonomic Sympathetic Discharge | Detlev Bronk, Univ. of Pennsylvania | Records continuous rhythmic electrical discharge along single postganglionic sympathetic nerve fibers. | `bci_tech` |

| 684 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1942 | Stephen Kuffler: Synaptic Potentials at Neuromuscular | Stephen Kuffler, Sydney & Baltimore | Isolates single nerve-muscle fiber pairs, resolving local non-propagated endplate potentials (EPPs). | `brain_basics` |

| 688 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1944 | Aristides Leao: Cortical Spreading Depression Discovered | Aristides Azevedo Pacheco Leao, Harvard Medical School | Discovers a slow 3 mm/min wave of electrical silencing propagating across rabbit cortex—the basis of migraine aura! | `brain_basics` |
| 689 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1944 | Erlanger and Gasser: Nobel Prize in Physiology | Joseph Erlanger & Herbert Gasser | Nobel Prize recognizes discovery of differentiated functional fiber types in nerve conduction velocities. | `brain_history` |

| 691 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1944 | Schrodinger: What is Life and Aperiodic Neural Crystals | Erwin Schrodinger, Dublin | Theoretical physicist proposes quantum genetic codes storing molecular memory inside neurons. | `brain_mysteries` |

| 693 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1945 | Unit 731: Lethal Japanese Hypothermia Brain Trials | Shiro Ishii, Harbin Manchuria | Horrific military human experiments freeze prisoners to death to study cerebral edema and revival limits. | `bci_ethics` |
| 694 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1945 | Walter Freeman: Transorbital Ice-Pick Lobotomy Invented | Walter Freeman, Washington DC | Drives modified ice-pick through orbital roof into prefrontal lobes with hammer: quick lobotomy born. | `bci_ethics` |
| 695 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1946 | Freeman Lobotomobile Tours: 3,500 Patients Severed | Walter Freeman, US Road Tours | Drives across state asylums, demonstrating 10-minute ice-pick lobotomies on schizophrenics and children. | `bci_ethics` |
| 696 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1946 | George Bishop: Fiber Diameter Correlates to Conduction Speed | George Bishop, Washington Univ. | Validates electrical physics: thick myelinated A-alpha axons conduct exponentially faster than thin C fibers. | `brain_basics` |

| 706 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1948 | Claude Shannon: Mathematical Theory of Information | Claude Shannon, Bell Labs | Defines entropy and bits, providing mathematical foundation for neural spike train information coding. | `bci_tech` |

| 715 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1950 | Alan Turing: Computing Machinery and the Imitation Game | Alan Turing, Univ. of Manchester | Mind paper proposes Turing Test, asking whether digital computers can replicate human cognitive processes. | `brain_mysteries` |
| 716 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1950 | Geoffrey Dawes: Development of Fetal Brain Autonomic | Geoffrey Dawes, Oxford Nuffield | Tracks onset of breathing and autonomic heart rate variability in mammalian fetal brains in utero. | `brain_basics` |
| 717 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1950 | Karl Lashley: In Search of the Engram Reflections | Karl Lashley, Harvard University | Concludes four decades of search failed to find localized memory trace in cortex, asserting distributed storage. | `brain_mysteries` |
| 718 | 6. Early 20th Century | 1950 | Paul Charpentier: Synthesis of Chlorpromazine (Thorazine) | Paul Charpentier, Rhone-Poulenc | Synthesizes aliphatic phenothiazine compound RP-4560, destined to trigger psychiatric drug revolution. | `brain_health` |

| 808 | 7. Mid 20th Century | 1966 | Saul Sternberg: High-Speed Exhaustive Memory Scanning | Saul Sternberg, Bell Telephone Labs | Reaction times increase by 38 milliseconds per digit held in working memory during recognition scans. | `brain_basics` |

| 836 | 7. Mid 20th Century | 1971 | John OKeefe: Discovery of Hippocampal Place Cells | John OKeefe & Jonathan Dostrovsky, UCL | Single-unit recordings in moving rats reveal pyramidal neurons firing only when animal occupies specific place in room. | `brain_basics` |
